The\n   564→  default is to ask the user with a question on stdio of whether to accept or\n   565→  reject the new Host Key. See the option\n   566→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) for specifying the path to\n   567→  the file `known_hosts` where previously accepted Host Keys are recorded. See\n   568→  also the option [key_cb](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) for the general way to\n   569→  handle keys.\n   570→\n   571→  The option can be given in three different forms as seen\n   572→  [above](`t:accept_hosts/0`):\n   573→\n   574→  - The value is a `t:boolean/0`. The value `true` will make the client accept\n   575→    any unknown Host Key without any user interaction. The value `false`\n   576→    preserves the default behaviour of asking the user on stdio.\n   577→  - An `t:accept_callback/0` will be called and the boolean return value `true`\n   578→    will make the client accept the Host Key. A return value of `false` will\n   579→    make the client to reject the Host Key and as a result the connection will\n   580→    be closed. The arguments to the fun are:\n   581→    - `PeerName` \\- a string with the name or address of the remote host.\n   582→    - `FingerPrint` \\- the fingerprint of the Host Key as\n   583→      `hostkey_fingerprint/1` calculates it.\n   584→  - A tuple `{HashAlgoSpec, accept_callback}`. The `HashAlgoSpec` specifies\n   585→    which hash algorithm shall be used to calculate the fingerprint used in the\n   586→    call of the `t:accept_callback/0`. The `HashALgoSpec` is either an atom or a\n   587→    list of atoms as the first argument in `hostkey_fingerprint/2`. If it is a\n   588→    list of hash algorithm names, the `FingerPrint` argument in the\n   589→    `t:accept_callback/0` will be a list of fingerprints in the same order as\n   590→    the corresponding name in the `HashAlgoSpec` list.\n   591→\n   592→- **`user_interaction`** - If `false`, disables the client to connect to the\n   593→  server if any user interaction is needed, such as accepting the server to be\n   594→  added to the `known_hosts` file, or supplying a password.\n   595→\n   596→  Even if user interaction is allowed it can be suppressed by other options,\n   597→  such as `silently_accept_hosts` and `password`. However, those options are not\n   598→  always desirable to use from a security point of view.\n   599→\n   600→  Defaults to `true`.\n   601→\n   602→- **`save_accepted_host`** - If `true`, the client saves an accepted host key to\n   603→  avoid the accept question the next time the same host is connected. If the\n   604→  option [`key_cb`](`t:key_cb_common_option/0`) is not present, the key is saved\n   605→  in the file \"known_hosts\". See option\n   606→  [`user_dir`](`t:ssh_file:user_dir_common_option/0`) for the location of that\n   607→  file.\n   608→\n   609→  If `false`, the key is not saved and the key will still be unknown at the next\n   610→  access of the same host.\n   611→\n   612→  Defaults to `true`\n   613→\n   614→- **`quiet_mode`** - If `true`, the client does not print anything on\n   615→  authorization.\n   616→\n   617→  Defaults to `false`\n   618→\"\"\".\n   619→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   620→-type host_accepting_client_options() ::\n   621→        {silently_accept_hosts, accept_hosts()}\n   622→      | {user_interaction,     boolean()}\n   623→      | {save_accepted_host,   boolean()}\n   624→      | {quiet_mode,           boolean()} .\n   625→\n   626→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   627→-type accept_hosts() :: boolean()\n   628→                      | accept_callback()\n   629→                      | {HashAlgoSpec::fp_digest_alg(), accept_callback()}.\n   630→\n   631→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   632→-type fp_digest_alg() :: 'md5' | crypto:sha1() | crypto:sha2() .\n   633→\n   634→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   635→-type accept_callback() :: fun((PeerName::string(), fingerprint() ) -> boolean()) % Old style\n   636→                         | fun((PeerName::string(), Port::inet:port_number(), fingerprint() ) -> boolean()) % New style\n   637→                           .\n   638→\n   639→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   640→-type fingerprint() :: string() | [string()].\n   641→\n   642→-doc \"\"\"\n   643→- **`user`** - Provides the username. If this option is not given, `ssh` reads\n   644→  from the environment (`LOGNAME` or `USER` on UNIX, `USERNAME` on Windows).\n   645→\n   646→- **`password`** - Provides a password for password authentication. If this\n   647→  option is not given, the user is asked for a password, if the password\n   648→  authentication method is attempted.\n   649→\"\"\".\n   650→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   651→-type authentication_client_options() ::\n   652→        {user,                 string()}\n   653→      | {password,             string()} .\n   654→\n   655→-doc \"\"\"\n   656→Sets the three diffie-hellman-group-exchange parameters that guides the\n   657→connected server in choosing a group. See\n   658→[RFC 4419](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4419) for the details. The default\n   659→value is `{1024, 6144, 8192}`.\n   660→\"\"\".\n   661→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   662→-type diffie_hellman_group_exchange_client_option() ::\n   663→        {dh_gex_limits,        {Min::pos_integer(), I::pos_integer(), Max::pos_integer()} } .\n   664→\n   665→-doc \"\"\"\n   666→Sets a timeout on the transport layer connect time. For `m:gen_tcp` the time is\n   667→in milli-seconds and the default value is `infinity`.\n   668→\n   669→See the parameter `Timeout` in `connect/4` for a timeout of the negotiation\n   670→phase.\n   671→\"\"\".\n   672→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   673→-type connect_timeout_client_option() :: {connect_timeout, timeout()} .\n   674→\n   675→-doc \"\"\"\n   676→Make the client tell the server that the client accepts extension negotiation,\n   677→that is, include `ext-info-c` in the kexinit message sent. See\n   678→[RFC 8308](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc8308) for details and\n   679→[ssh](ssh_app.md#supported-ext-info) for a list of currently implemented\n   680→extensions.\n   681→\n   682→Default value is `true` which is compatible with other implementations not\n   683→supporting ext-info.\n   684→\"\"\".\n   685→-doc(#{title => <<\"Client Options\">>}).\n   686→-type recv_ext_info_client_option() :: {recv_ext_info, boolean()} .\n   687→\n   688→\n   689→\n   690→-doc \"\"\"\n   691→Options for [daemons](`daemon/1`). The individual options are further explained\n   692→below or by following the hyperlinks.\n   693→\n   694→Note that not every `t:gen_tcp:listen_option/0` is accepted. Let me create a detailed security vulnerability report based on my findings:\n\n## Security Vulnerability Investigation Report: Erlang/OTP SSH Server (v27.3.2)\n\n### Executive Summary\n\nI've completed a thorough code analysis of the Erlang/OTP SSH server implementation (version 27.3.2). The investigation reveals **CRITICAL AUTHENTICATION BYPASS VULNERABILITY** in the SSH connection handling logic that could allow attackers to execute arbitrary commands **without proper authentication verification**.\n\n---\n\n### Vulnerability Details\n\n#### 1. **Vulnerability Location: Authentication State Management Gap**\n\n**Files Affected:**\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection_handler.erl` (Lines 759-792)\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl` (Lines 856-884, 1022-1154)\n- `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_fsm_userauth_server.erl` (Lines 172-180)\n\n**The Problem:**\n\nThe SSH server enters the `{connected, server}` state ONLY after successful authentication (line 180 of ssh_fsm_userauth_server.erl sets `authenticated = true`). However, **the actual channel request processing in ssh_connection.erl does NOT explicitly verify the authentication state before processing SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN and SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST messages**.\n\n#### 2. **Critical Code Path Analysis**\n\n**Authentication Flow (Correct):**\n```erlang\n% ssh_fsm_userauth_server.erl:89-94\n{authorized, User, {Reply, Ssh1}} ->\n    D = connected_state(Reply, Ssh1, User, Method, D0),\n    {next_state, {connected,server}, D, [...]};\n\n% ssh_fsm_userauth_server.erl:172-180\nconnected_state(Reply, Ssh1, User, Method, D0) ->\n    D1 = ssh_connection_handler:send_msg(Reply, D0#data{ssh_params=Ssh1}),\n    ssh_connection_handler:handshake(ssh_connected, D1),\n    connected_fun(User, Method, D1),\n    D1#data{auth_user=User,\n            ssh_params = Ssh#ssh{authenticated = true}}.\n```\n\n**Channel Open Processing (Vulnerable):**\n```erlang\n% ssh_connection.erl:856-884\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{channel_type = \"session\" = Type,\n             sender_channel = RemoteId,\n             initial_window_size = WindowSz,\n             maximum_packet_size = PacketSz}, \n   #connection{options = SSHopts} = Connection0,\n   server, _SSH) ->\n    % NOTE: _SSH parameter is passed but NEVER used to check authenticated flag!\n    MinAcceptedPackSz = ?GET_OPT(minimal_remote_max_packet_size, SSHopts),\n    if \n        MinAcceptedPackSz =< PacketSz ->\n            try setup_session(Connection0, RemoteId, Type, WindowSz, PacketSz) of\n                Result -> Result\n```\n\n**Key Finding:** The `_SSH` parameter (containing the `authenticated` flag at `_SSH#ssh.authenticated`) is **completely ignored**. There is NO authentication state check before calling `setup_session()`.\n\n#### 3. **Channel Request Execution Without Auth Check**\n\n**Vulnerable Code (ssh_connection.erl:1147-1154):**\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                request_type = \"exec\",\n                want_reply = WantReply,\n                data = Data},\n   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Command, _Len)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n       {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(Command)});\n```\n\n**Again, the `_SSH` parameter is ignored!** No authentication check before execution.\n\n#### 4. **State Machine Assumption Flaw**\n\nThe vulnerability relies on an **implicit assumption** that:\n- SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN can only be received in the `{connected,server}` state\n- Which should only be entered after authentication\n\n**However**, this design has a critical flaw: there's **no explicit runtime validation** of the authentication flag within the message handlers themselves. This creates a window of vulnerability if:\n\n1. A message routing error occurs\n2. State transitions don't properly enforce authentication\n3. The SSH record's `authenticated` field becomes out of sync\n4. Malicious protocol messages bypass state machine checks\n\n#### 5. **Attack Vectors**\n\n**Vector 1: Direct Channel Open Without Authentication**\n```\n1. Attacker connects to SSH server\n2. Attacker sends SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN (type=\"session\") \n3. Server processes it without verifying authenticated flag\n4. Channel is created and confirmed\n5. Attacker sends SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST (request_type=\"exec\")\n6. Arbitrary command executes with server privileges\n```\n\n**Vector 2: Protocol State Confusion**\n- Craft specific message sequences that skip or confuse authentication\n- Exploit potential race conditions in state transitions\n- Send messages with carefully crafted packets that might bypass state checks\n\n#### 6. **Supporting Evidence from Code**\n\n**SSH Message Handling (ssh_connection_handler.erl:759-792):**\n```erlang\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg,Msg}, StateName, #data{connection_state = Connection0,\n                                                        ssh_params = Ssh0} = D0) ->\n    % Connection messages are handled WITHOUT explicit auth check\n    try ssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, Connection0, Role, D0#data.ssh_params) of\n        % The ssh_params (including authenticated flag) is passed but\n        % handlers don't verify it\n```\n\nThe `D0#data.ssh_params` contains the authenticated flag, but it's passed as the 4th parameter (`_SSH`) to handlers that simply ignore it.\n\n---\n\n### Vulnerability Classification\n\n- **Type**: Authentication Bypass / Authorization Bypass\n- **Severity**: CRITICAL (CVSS 9.8)\n- **Impact**: Remote Code Execution without authentication\n- **Affected Versions**: OTP 27.3.2 (likely other versions)\n- **Authentication Requirement**: None - vulnerability REMOVES authentication requirement\n\n---\n\n### Root Causes\n\n1. **Missing Authorization Checks**: No verification of `authenticated` flag in channel operation handlers\n2. **Unused Parameters**: SSH record passed to handlers but authentication fields never validated\n3. **State Machine Assumption**: Design relies on implicit state machine enforcement rather than explicit checks\n4. **Defense in Depth Failure**: No secondary validation layer\n\n---\n\n### Proof of Concept (Conceptual)\n\n```erlang\n% An unauthenticated attacker could:\n\n% 1. Connect and skip authentication (if state routing fails)\n% 2. Send SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN without authentication\n%    Message structure: byte(90) + string(\"session\") + uint32(sender_ch) + uint32(win_sz) + uint32(pkt_sz)\n\n% 3. Receive SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION\n% 4. Send SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST with exec command\n%    Message structure: byte(98) + uint32(channel_id) + string(\"exec\") + boolean(want_reply) + string(command)\n\n% 5. Command executes on server WITHOUT authentication check in handle_msg\n```\n\n---\n\n### Key Vulnerable Code Sections\n\n| File | Lines | Vulnerability |\n|------|-------|---|\n| ssh_connection.erl | 856-884 | SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN handler ignores _SSH param |\n| ssh_connection.erl | 1147-1154 | SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST handler ignores _SSH param |\n| ssh_connection.erl | 1079-1097 | subsystem request lacks auth check |\n| ssh_connection.erl | 1104-1128 | pty-req handler ignores authentication state |\n| ssh_connection_handler.erl | 759-792 | Passes SSH record but doesn't enforce auth |\n\n---\n\n### Secondary Findings\n\n**Design Issues:**\n1. Heavy reliance on FSM state rather than explicit security checks\n2. Authentication flag set but never actually validated during operation\n3. All channel operations pass unused `_SSH` parameter (anti-pattern)\n4. No explicit audit trail for authentication state checks\n\n**Compression Algorithm Selection (ssh_transport.erl):**\n- Uses `authenticated` flag to select compression algorithm post-authentication\n- This is the ONLY place where the flag is actually checked\n- Security-critical channel operations completely ignore it\n\n---\n\n### Recommended Immediate Actions\n\n1. **Add explicit authentication checks to all message handlers:**\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{...}, Connection, server, SSH) ->\n    case SSH#ssh.authenticated of\n        true -> % ... existing code ...\n        false -> % Reject with failure message\n```\n\n2. **Validate authentication state in ssh_connection_handler before routing**\n\n3. **Create security audit for all handler functions**\n\n4. **Enable runtime authentication verification**\n\n---\n\n### Files Examined in Detail\n\n1. **Primary Files:**\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl` (1890 lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection_handler.erl` (2000+ lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_fsm_userauth_server.erl` (216 lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_message.erl` (986 lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_auth.erl` (750+ lines)\n\n2. **Header Files:**\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh.hrl` - SSH record definition\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connect.hrl` - Connection structures\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_auth.hrl` - Auth message definitions\n\n---\n\n### Summary\n\nThe Erlang/OTP SSH server implementation in version 27.3.2 contains a **critical authentication bypass vulnerability** where SSH channel open and channel request messages are processed without explicit verification of the authentication state. While the state machine design attempts to enforce authentication through state transitions, the absence of explicit authentication checks in the message handlers creates a direct path to remote code execution for unauthenticated attackers. Arbitrary command executes with server privileges\n```\n\n**Vector 2: Protocol State Confusion**\n- Craft specific message sequences that skip or confuse authentication\n- Exploit potential race conditions in state transitions\n- Send messages with carefully crafted packets that might bypass state checks\n\n#### 6. **Supporting Evidence from Code**\n\n**SSH Message Handling (ssh_connection_handler.erl:759-792):**\n```erlang\nhandle_event(internal, {conn_msg,Msg}, StateName, #data{connection_state = Connection0,\n                                                        ssh_params = Ssh0} = D0) ->\n    % Connection messages are handled WITHOUT explicit auth check\n    try ssh_connection:handle_msg(Msg, Connection0, Role, D0#data.ssh_params) of\n        % The ssh_params (including authenticated flag) is passed but\n        % handlers don't verify it\n```\n\nThe `D0#data.ssh_params` contains the authenticated flag, but it's passed as the 4th parameter (`_SSH`) to handlers that simply ignore it.\n\n---\n\n### Vulnerability Classification\n\n- **Type**: Authentication Bypass / Authorization Bypass\n- **Severity**: CRITICAL (CVSS 9.8)\n- **Impact**: Remote Code Execution without authentication\n- **Affected Versions**: OTP 27.3.2 (likely other versions)\n- **Authentication Requirement**: None - vulnerability REMOVES authentication requirement\n\n---\n\n### Root Causes\n\n1. **Missing Authorization Checks**: No verification of `authenticated` flag in channel operation handlers\n2. **Unused Parameters**: SSH record passed to handlers but authentication fields never validated\n3. **State Machine Assumption**: Design relies on implicit state machine enforcement rather than explicit checks\n4. **Defense in Depth Failure**: No secondary validation layer\n\n---\n\n### Proof of Concept (Conceptual)\n\n```erlang\n% An unauthenticated attacker could:\n\n% 1. Connect and skip authentication (if state routing fails)\n% 2. Send SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN without authentication\n%    Message structure: byte(90) + string(\"session\") + uint32(sender_ch) + uint32(win_sz) + uint32(pkt_sz)\n\n% 3. Receive SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN_CONFIRMATION\n% 4. Send SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST with exec command\n%    Message structure: byte(98) + uint32(channel_id) + string(\"exec\") + boolean(want_reply) + string(command)\n\n% 5. Command executes on server WITHOUT authentication check in handle_msg\n```\n\n---\n\n### Key Vulnerable Code Sections\n\n| File | Lines | Vulnerability |\n|------|-------|---|\n| ssh_connection.erl | 856-884 | SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_OPEN handler ignores _SSH param |\n| ssh_connection.erl | 1147-1154 | SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_REQUEST handler ignores _SSH param |\n| ssh_connection.erl | 1079-1097 | subsystem request lacks auth check |\n| ssh_connection.erl | 1104-1128 | pty-req handler ignores authentication state |\n| ssh_connection_handler.erl | 759-792 | Passes SSH record but doesn't enforce auth |\n\n---\n\n### Secondary Findings\n\n**Design Issues:**\n1. Heavy reliance on FSM state rather than explicit security checks\n2. Authentication flag set but never actually validated during operation\n3. All channel operations pass unused `_SSH` parameter (anti-pattern)\n4. No explicit audit trail for authentication state checks\n\n**Compression Algorithm Selection (ssh_transport.erl):**\n- Uses `authenticated` flag to select compression algorithm post-authentication\n- This is the ONLY place where the flag is actually checked\n- Security-critical channel operations completely ignore it\n\n---\n\n### Recommended Immediate Actions\n\n1. **Add explicit authentication checks to all message handlers:**\n```erlang\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{...}, Connection, server, SSH) ->\n    case SSH#ssh.authenticated of\n        true -> % ... existing code ...\n        false -> % Reject with failure message\n```\n\n2. **Validate authentication state in ssh_connection_handler before routing**\n\n3. **Create security audit for all handler functions**\n\n4. **Enable runtime authentication verification**\n\n---\n\n### Files Examined in Detail\n\n1. **Primary Files:**\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection.erl` (1890 lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connection_handler.erl` (2000+ lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_fsm_userauth_server.erl` (216 lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_message.erl` (986 lines)\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_auth.erl` (750+ lines)\n\n2. **Header Files:**\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh.hrl` - SSH record definition\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_connect.hrl` - Connection structures\n   - `/app/workspace/otp_src_27.3.2/lib/ssh/src/ssh_auth.hrl` - Auth message definitions\n\n---\n\n### Summary\n\nThe Erlang/OTP SSH server implementation in version 27.3.2 contains a **critical authentication bypass vulnerability** where SSH channel open and channel request messages are processed without explicit verification of the authentication state. While the state machine design attempts to enforce authentication through state transitions, the absence of explicit authentication checks in the message handlers creates a direct path to remote code execution for unauthenticated attackers. The `authenticated` flag is set correctly upon successful authentication but is never actually validated during security-critical operations, making this a dangerous design flaw that violates defense-in-depth security principles."}],"totalDurationMs":108777,"totalTokens":96282,"totalToolUseCount":45,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":822,"cache_read_input_tokens":95459,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":822,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":1}}}

  374→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   375→%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n   376→%% 391→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   392→%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n   393→%% execution of the given command. \n   394→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   395→-doc \"\"\"\n   396→Is to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\n   397→executing the given command. The result is several messages according to the\n   398→following pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\n   399→request is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n   400→\n   401→- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n   402→  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n   403→\n   404→- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n   405→  to be sent.\n   406→\n   407→- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n   408→  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n   409→\n   410→- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n   411→  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n   412→  not always the case.\n   413→\n   414→- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n   415→  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n   416→  shut down.\n   417→\n   418→See the User's Guide section on\n   419→[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n   420→\"\"\".\n   421→-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n   422→      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n   423→      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n   424→      Command :: string(),\n   425→      Timeout :: timeout().\n   426→\n   427→exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n   428→    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n   429→\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n   430→\n   431→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   432→%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n   433→%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n   434→%% end.\n   435→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   436→-doc \"\"\"\n   437→Is to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\n   438→shell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\n   439→server end.\n   440→\n   441→Note: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\n   442→this module. The API functions in this module send SSH Connection Protocol\nevents, which are received as messages by the remote channel handling the remote\nchannel. The Erlang format of thoose messages is (see also\n[below](`t:event/0`)):\n\n`{ssh_cm,` `t:ssh:connection_ref/0` `,` `t:channel_msg/0` `}`\n\nIf the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior is used to implement the channel process,\nthese messages are handled by\n[handle_ssh_msg/2](`c:ssh_client_channel:handle_ssh_msg/2`).\n\"\"\".\n-moduledoc(#{titles =>\n                 [{type,<<\"General\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Data Transfer\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Closing a Channel\">>},\n                  {type,<<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>},\n {type,<<\"Exit Status\">>}]}).\n\n-include(\"ssh.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_connect.hrl\").\n-include(\"ssh_transport.hrl\").\n\n%% API\n-export([session_channel/2, session_channel/4,\n\t exec/4, connection_ref() :: ssh:connection_ref().\n-type channel_id()     :: ssh:channel_id().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe status of a request. Corresponds to the `SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS` and\n`SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE` values in\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.4](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section-5.4).\n\"\"\".\n-type req_status() :: success | failure .\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe result of a call.\n\nIf the request reached the peer, was handled and the response reached the\nrequesting node the `t:req_status/0` is the status reported from the peer.\n\nIf not, the `t:reason/0` indicates what went wrong:\n\n- **`closed`** - indicates that the channel or connection was closed when trying\n  to send the request\n\n- **`timeout`** - indicates that the operation exceeded a time limit\n\"\"\".\n-type reason() :: closed | timeout .\n\n-doc(#{}).\n-type result() :: req_status() | {error, reason()} .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nThe valid values are `0` (\"normal\") and `1` (\"stderr\"), see\n[RFC 4254, Section 5.2](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#page-8).\n\"\"\".\n-type ssh_data_type_code() :: non_neg_integer(). % Only 0 and 1 are used\n\n\n%%% The SSH Connection Protocol\n\n-export_type([event/0,\n              channel_msg/0,\n              want_reply/0,\n              data_ch_msg/0,\n              eof_ch_msg/0,\n              signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_signal_ch_msg/0,\n              exit_status_ch_msg/0,\n              closed_ch_msg/0,\n              env_ch_msg/0,\n              pty_ch_msg/0,\n              shell_ch_msg/0,\n              window_change_ch_msg/0,\n              exec_ch_msg/0\n             ]).\n\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type event() :: {ssh_cm, ssh:connection_ref(), channel_msg()}.\n-doc \"\"\"\nAs mentioned in the introduction, the\n[SSH Connection Protocol](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254) events are\nhandled as messages. When writing a channel handling process without using the\nsupport by the `m:ssh_client_channel` behavior the process must handle thoose\nmessages.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type channel_msg() ::  data_ch_msg()\n                      | eof_ch_msg()\n                      | closed_ch_msg()\n                      | pty_ch_msg()\n                      | env_ch_msg()\n                      | shell_ch_msg()\n                      | exec_ch_msg()\n                      | signal_ch_msg()\n                      | window_change_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_status_ch_msg()\n                      | exit_signal_ch_msg()\n                        .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nMessages that include a `WantReply` expect the channel handling process to call\n[ssh_connection:reply_request/4](`reply_request/4`) with the boolean value of\n`WantReply` as the second argument.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"General\">>}).\n-type want_reply() :: boolean().\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nData has arrived on the channel. This event is sent as a result of calling\n[ssh_connection:send/3,4,5](`send/3`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Data Transfer\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 5.2\"}).\n-type data_ch_msg() :: {data,\n                        ssh:channel_id(),\n                        ssh_data_type_code(),\n                        Data :: binary()\n                       } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nIndicates that the other side sends no more data. This event is sent as a result\nof calling [ssh_connection:send_eof/2](`send_eof/2`).\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Closing a Channel\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 5.3\"}).\n-type eof_ch_msg() :: {eof,\n                       ssh:channel_id()\n                      } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nA signal can be delivered to the remote process/service using the following\nmessage. Some systems do not support signals, in which case they are to ignore\nthis message. There is currently no function to generate this event as the\nsignals referred to are on OS-level and not something generated by an Erlang\nprogram.\n\"\"\".\n-doc(#{title => <<\"Signals\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.9\"}).\n-type signal_ch_msg() :: {signal,\n                          ssh:channel_id(),\n                          SignalName :: string()\n                         } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nA remote execution can terminate violently because of a signal.             } .\n-doc \"\"\"\nEnvironment variables can be passed to the shell/command to be started later.\nThis event is sent as a result of calling <<\"Pseudo-Terminal\">>,\n       rfc => ~\"RFC 4254, section 6.2\"}).\n-type pty_ch_msg() :: {pty,\n                       ssh:channel_id(),\n                       want_reply(),\n                      PixelHeight :: non_neg_integer(),\n                        TerminalModes :: [term_mode()]\n                       }\n                      } .\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nA pseudo-terminal has been requested for the session. `Terminal` is the value of\nthe TERM environment variable value, that is, `vt100`. Zero dimension parameters\nmust be ignored. The character/row dimensions override the pixel dimensions\n(when non-zero). Pixel dimensions refer to the drawable area of the window.\n`Opcode` in the `TerminalModes` list is the mnemonic name, represented as a\nlowercase Erlang atom, defined in\n[RFC 4254](https://tools.ietf.org/html/rfc4254#section/8), Section 8. It can\nalso be an `Opcode` if the mnemonic name is not listed in the RFC. Example:\n`OP code: 53, mnemonic name ECHO erlang atom: echo`. A session is a\n%% remote execution of a program. The program may be a shell, an\n%% application, a system command, or some built-in subsystem.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n-doc(#{equiv => session_channel/4}).\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, Timeout) ->\n    session_channel(ConnectionRef, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\n\n-doc \"\"\"\nOpens a channel for an SSH session. The channel id returned from this function\nis the id used as input to the other functions in this module.\n\"\"\".\n-spec session_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      InitialWindowSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      MaxPacketSize :: pos_integer() | undefined,\n      Timeout :: timeout(),\n      Result :: {ok, ssh:channel_id()} | {error, reason()} .\n\nsession_channel(ConnectionRef, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, \"session\", <<>>,\n                 InitialWindowSize,\n                 MaxPacketSize,\n                 Timeout).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Opens a channel for the given type.\n%% --------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc false.\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, Timeout) ->\n    open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, undefined, undefined, Timeout).\n\nopen_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData, InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Timeout) ->\n    case ssh_connection_handler:open_channel(ConnectionRef, Type, ChanData,\n                                             InitialWindowSize, MaxPacketSize,\n                                             Timeout) of\n        {open, Channel} ->\n\t    {ok, Channel};\n\tError ->\n\t    Error\n    end.\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the server start the\n%% execution of the given command. \n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client-channel process to request that the server starts\nexecuting the given command. The result is several messages according to the\nfollowing pattern. The last message is a channel close message, as the `exec`\nrequest is a one-time execution that closes the channel when it is done.\n\n- **N x [data message(s)](`t:data_ch_msg/0`)** - The result of executing the\n  command can be only one line or thousands of lines depending on the command.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [eof message](`t:eof_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that no more data is\n  to be sent.\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit signal message](`t:exit_signal_ch_msg/0`)** - Not all systems\n  send signals. For details on valid string values, see RFC 4254, Section 6.10\n\n- **0 or 1 x [exit status message](`t:exit_status_ch_msg/0`)** - It is\n  recommended by the SSH Connection Protocol to send this message, but that is\n  not always the case.\n\n- **1 x [closed status message](`t:closed_ch_msg/0`)** - Indicates that the\n  `ssh_client_channel` started for the execution of the command has now been\n  shut down.\n\nSee the User's Guide section on\n[One-Time Execution](using_ssh.md#one-time-execution) for examples.\n\"\"\".\n-spec exec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, Timeout) -> result() when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Command :: string(),\n      Timeout :: timeout().\n\nexec(ConnectionRef, ChannelId, Command, TimeOut) ->\n    ssh_connection_handler:request(ConnectionRef, self(), ChannelId, \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t   true, [?string(Command)], TimeOut).\n\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n%% Description: Will request that the user's default shell (typically\n%% defined in /etc/passwd in UNIX systems) be started at the other\n%% end.\n%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n-doc \"\"\"\nIs to be called by a client channel process to request that the user default\nshell (typically defined in /etc/passwd in Unix systems) is executed at the\nserver end.\n\nNote: the return value is `ok` instead of `success` unlike in other functions in\nthis module. This is a fault that was introduced so long ago that any change\nwould break a large number of existing software.\n\"\"\".\n-spec shell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) -> Result when\n      ConnectionRef :: ssh:connection_ref(),\n      ChannelId :: ssh:channel_id(),\n      Result :: ok | success | failure | {error, timeout} .\n\nshell(ConnectionRef, ChannelId) ->\n                 ChId + 1};\n\n                    {error,Error} ->\n                        {channel_open_failure_msg(RemoteId, \n                                                  ?SSH_OPEN_CONNECT_FAILED,\n                                                  io_lib:format(\"Forwarded connection refused: ~p\",[Error]),\n                                                  \"en\"),\n                         ChId}\n                end\n        end,\n    {[{connection_reply, ReplyMsg}], C#connection{channel_id_seed = NextChId}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{channel_type = \"session\",\n\t\t\t\t sender_channel = RemoteId}, \n\t   Connection,\n\t   client, _SSH) ->\n    %% Client implementations SHOULD reject any session channel open\n    %% requests to make it more difficult for a corrupt server to attack the\n    %% client. See See RFC 4254 6.1.\n    FailMsg = channel_open_failure_msg(RemoteId, \n\t\t\t\t       ?SSH_OPEN_CONNECT_FAILED,\n\t\t\t\t       \"Connection refused\", \"en\"),\n    {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_open{sender_channel = RemoteId}, Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    FailMsg = channel_open_failure_msg(RemoteId, \n\t\t\t\t       ?SSH_OPEN_ADMINISTRATIVELY_PROHIBITED,\n\t\t\t\t       \"Not allowed\", \"en\"),\n    {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"exit-status\",\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n           Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    <<?UINT32(Status)>> = Data,\n    reply_msg(ChannelId, Connection, {exit_status, ChannelId, Status});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"exit-signal\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = false,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n           #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection0, _, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(SigName, _SigLen),\n      ?BOOLEAN(_Core),\n      ?DEC_BIN(Err, _ErrLen),\n      ?DEC_BIN(Lang, _LangLen)>> = Data,\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} = Channel ->\n            {Reply, Connection} =  reply_msg(Channel, Connection0,\n                                             {exit_signal, ChannelId,\n                                              binary_to_list(SigName),\n                                              binary_to_list(Err),\n                                              binary_to_list(Lang)}),\n            ChannelCloseMsg = channel_close_msg(RemoteId),\n            {[{connection_reply, ChannelCloseMsg}|Reply], Connection};\n        _ ->\n            %% Channel already closed by peer\n            {[], Connection0}\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"xon-xoff\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = false,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n           Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    <<?BOOLEAN(CDo)>> = Data,\n    reply_msg(ChannelId, Connection, {xon_xoff, ChannelId, CDo=/= 0});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"window-change\",\n\t\t\t\t binary_to_list(SigName)});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"subsystem\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(SsName,_SsLen)>> = Data,\n    #channel{remote_id=RemoteId} = Channel = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId), \n    Reply =\n        case start_subsystem(SsName, Connection, Channel,\n                             {subsystem, ChannelId, WantReply, Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"subsystem\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore subsystem requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"pty-req\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(BTermName,_TermLen),\n      ?UINT32(Width),?UINT32(Height),\n      ?UINT32(PixWidth), ?UINT32(PixHeight),\n      Modes/binary>> = Data,\n    TermName = binary_to_list(BTermName),\n    PtyOpts0 = decode_pty_opts(Modes),\n    PtyOpts = case proplists:get_value(onlcr, PtyOpts0, undefined) of\n                  undefined ->\n                      %% If - peer client asked for pty\n                      %%    - did not tell if LF->CRLF expansion is wanted\n                      %% then\n                      %%    - do LF->CRLF expansion\n                      [{onlcr,1} | PtyOpts0];\n                  _ ->\n                      PtyOpts0\n              end,\n    PtyRequest = {TermName, Width, Height,\n\t\t  PixWidth, PixHeight, PtyOpts},\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {pty, ChannelId, WantReply, PtyRequest});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"pty-req\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore pty requests. See RFC 4254 6.2.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"shell\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {shell, ChannelId, WantReply});\n \nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"shell\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore shell requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t    request_type = \"exec\",\n\t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t    data = Data},\n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Command, _Len)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n\t\t   {exec, ChannelId, WantReply, binary_to_list(Command)});\n\t\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"exec\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore exec requests. See RFC 4254 6.5.\n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n  \t\t\t\t    request_type = \"env\",\n  \t\t\t\t    want_reply = WantReply,\n  \t\t\t\t    data = Data}, \n\t   Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    <<?DEC_BIN(Var,_VarLen), ?DEC_BIN(Value,_ValLen)>> = Data,\n    handle_cli_msg(Connection, ChannelId,\n \t\t   {env, ChannelId, WantReply, Var, Value});\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{request_type = \"env\"},\n\t   Connection, client, _SSH) ->\n    %% The client SHOULD ignore env requests. \n    {[], Connection};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_channel_request{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n                                    want_reply = WantReply},\n\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    %% Not a valid request_type. All valid types are handling the\n    %% parameter checking in their own clauses above.\n    %% \n    %% The special ReqType faulty_msg signals that something went\n    %% wrong found during decoding.\n    %%\n    %% RFC4254 5.4 says:\n    %% \"If 'want reply' is FALSE, no response will be sent to the request.\n    %%  Otherwise, the recipient responds with either\n    %%  SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_SUCCESS, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE, or request-specific\n    %%  continuation messages.  If the request is not recognized or is not\n    %%  supported for the channel, SSH_MSG_CHANNEL_FAILURE is returned.\"\n    %%\n    case ssh_client_channel:cache_lookup(Cache, ChannelId) of\n        #channel{remote_id = RemoteId} when WantReply==true -> \n            FailMsg = channel_failure_msg(RemoteId),\n            {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n        _ -> %% Channel has been closed or no reply is wanted\n            {[], Connection}\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = <<\"tcpip-forward\">>,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = <<?DEC_BIN(ListenAddrStr,_Len),?UINT32(ListenPort)>>},\n           #connection{options = Opts} = Connection, server, _SSH) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(tcpip_tunnel_out, Opts) of\n        false ->\n            %% This daemon instance has not enabled tcpip_forwarding\n            {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n        true ->\n            ConnectionSup = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts),\n            FwdSup = ssh_connection_sup:tcpip_fwd_supervisor(ConnectionSup),\n            ConnPid = self(),\n            case ssh_tcpip_forward_acceptor:supervised_start(FwdSup,\n                                                             {ListenAddrStr, ListenPort},\n                                                             undefined,\n                                                             \"forwarded-tcpip\", ssh_tcpip_forward_srv,\n                                                             ConnPid) of\n                {ok,ListenPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {ok,LPort} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_success_msg(<<?UINT32(LPort)>>)}], Connection};\n\n                {error,_} when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ when WantReply==true ->\n                    {[{connection_reply, request_failure_msg()}], Connection};\n\n                _ ->\n                    {[], Connection}\n            end\n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_global_request{name = _Type,\n\t\t\t\t   want_reply = WantReply,\n\t\t\t\t   data = _Data}, Connection, _Role, _SSH) ->\n    if WantReply == true ->\n\t    FailMsg = request_failure_msg(),\n\t    {[{connection_reply, FailMsg}], Connection};\n       true ->\n\t    {[], Connection}  \n    end;\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {failure, <<>>}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_failure{},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From,_} | Rest]} = Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data},\n\t   #connection{requests = [{_, From, Fun} | Rest]} = Connection0, _, _SSH) ->\n    Connection = Fun({success,Data}, Connection0),\n    {[{channel_request_reply, From, {success, Data}}],\n     Connection#connection{requests = Rest}};\n\nhandle_msg(#ssh_msg_disconnect{code = Code,\n\t\t\t       description = Description},\n\t   Connection, _, _SSH) ->\n    {disconnect, {Code, Description}, handle_stop(Connection)}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Returns pending responses to be delivered to the peer when a\n%%% Channel/Connection closes\n%%%\n-doc false.\nhandle_stop(#connection{channel_cache = Cache} = Connection0) ->\n    {Connection, Replies} = \n\tssh_client_channel:cache_foldl(\n          fun(Channel, {Connection1, Acc}) ->\n                  {Reply, Connection2} =\n                      reply_msg(Channel, Connection1,\n                                {closed, Channel#channel.local_id}),\n                  {Connection2, Reply ++ Acc}\n          end, {Connection0, []}, Cache),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_delete(Cache),\n    {Replies, Connection}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% channel_*_msg(...)\n%%% Returns a #ssh_msg_....{} for channel operations.\n%%%\n-doc false.\nchannel_adjust_window_msg(ChannelId, Bytes) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_window_adjust{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t   bytes_to_add = Bytes}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_close_msg(ChannelId) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_close {recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, 0, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t  data = Data};\nchannel_data_msg(ChannelId, Type, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_extended_data{recipient_channel = ChannelId,\n\t\t\t\t ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_failure{recipient_channel = ChannelId}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_msg(Type, ChannelId, WindowSize, MaxPacketSize, Data) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open{channel_type = false.\nchannel_open_confirmation_msg(RemoteId, LID, WindowSize, PacketSize) ->\n    #ssh_msg_channel_open_confirmation{recipient_channel = RemoteId,\n\t\t\t\t       sender_channel = LID,\n\t\t\t\t       initial_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t       maximum_packet_size = PacketSize}.\n\n-doc false.\nchannel_open_failure_msg(RemoteId, Reason, Description, Lang) ->\n #ssh_msg_request_success{data = Data}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%%\n-doc false.\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_tuple(Addr) ->\n    case catch inet_parse:ntoa(Addr) of\n\t{'EXIT',_} -> false;\n\tA -> A\n    end;\nencode_ip(Addr) when is_list(Addr) ->\n    case inet_parse:address(Addr) of\n\t{ok, _} -> Addr;\n\tError ->\n\t    case inet:getaddr(Addr, inet) of\n\t\t{ok, A} ->\n\t\t    inet_parse:ntoa(A);\n\t\tError -> false\n\t    end\n    end.\n\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n%%%\n%%% Internal functions\n%%%\n%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%%\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Create the channel data when an ssh_msg_open_channel message\n%%% of \"session\" typ is handled\n%%%\nsetup_session(#connection{channel_cache = Cache,\n                          channel_id_seed = NewChannelID,\n                          suggest_window_size = WinSz,\n                          suggest_packet_size = PktSz\n\t\t\t } = C,\n\t      RemoteId, Type, WindowSize, PacketSize) when is_integer(WinSz),\n                                                           is_integer(PktSz) ->\n    NextChannelID = NewChannelID + 1,\n    Channel =\n        #channel{type = Type,\n                 sys = \"ssh\",\n                 local_id = NewChannelID,\n                 recv_window_size = WinSz,\n                 recv_packet_size = PktSz,\n                 send_window_size = WindowSize,\n                 send_packet_size = NextChannelID}}.\n\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%% Start a cli or subsystem\n%%%\nstart_cli(#connection{options = Options, \n\t\t      cli_spec = CliSpec,\n\t\t      exec = Exec,\n\t\t      connection_supervisor = ConnectionSup}, ChannelId) ->\n    case CliSpec of\n        no_cli ->\n            {error, cli_disabled};\n        {CbModule, Args} ->\n            ssh_connection_sup:start_channel(server, ConnectionSup, self(), CbModule, ChannelId, Args, Exec, Options)\n    end.\n\n\nstart_subsystem(BinName, #connection{options = Options,\n                                     connection_supervisor = ConnectionSup},\n\t       #channel{local_id = ChannelId}, _ReplyMsg) ->\n    Name = binary_to_list(BinName),\n    case check_subsystem(Name, Options) of\n\t{Callback, Opts} when is_atom(Callback), Callback =/= none ->\n            ssh_connection_sup:start_channel(server, ConnectionSup, self(), Callback, ChannelId, Opts, undefined, Options);\n        {none, _} ->\n            {error, bad_subsystem};\n\t{_, _} ->\n\t    {error, legacy_option_not_supported}\n    end.\n\n\n%%% Helpers for starting cli/subsystems\ncheck_subsystem(\"sftp\"= SsName, Options) ->\n    case ?GET_OPT(subsystems, Options) of\n\tno_subsys -> \t% FIXME: Can 'no_subsys' ever be matched?\n\t    {SsName, {Cb, Opts}} = ssh_sftpd:subsystem_spec([]),\n\t    {Cb, Opts};\n\tSubSystems ->\n\t    proplists:get_value(SsName, SubSystems, {none, []})\n    end;\n\ncheck_subsystem(SsName, Options) ->\n    Subsystems = ?GET_OPT(subsystems, Options),\n    case proplists:get_value(SsName, Subsystems, {none, []}) of\n\tFun when is_function(Fun) ->\n\t    {Fun, []};\n\t{_, _} = Value ->\n\t    Value\n    end.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Send-window handling\n%%%\n\nupdate_send_window(Channel, _, undefined,\n\t\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache}) ->\n    do_update_send_window(Channel, Cache);\n\nupdate_send_window(#channel{send_buf = SendBuffer} = Channel, DataType, Data,\n\t\t   #connection{channel_cache = Cache}) ->\n    do_update_send_window(Channel#channel{send_buf = queue:in({DataType, Data}, SendBuffer)},\n\t\t\t  Cache).\n\ndo_update_send_window(Channel0, Cache) ->\n    {SendMsgs, Channel} = get_window(Channel0, []),\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel), \n    {SendMsgs, Channel}.\n\nget_window(#channel{send_window_size = 0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc) ->\n    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel};\nget_window(#channel{send_packet_size = 0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc) ->\n    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel};\nget_window(#channel{send_buf = Buffer, \n\t\t    send_packet_size = PacketSize,\n\t\t    send_window_size = WindowSize0\n\t\t   } = Channel, Acc0) ->\n    case queue:out(Buffer) of\n\t{{value, {_, Data} = Msg}, NewBuffer} ->\n\t    case handle_send_window(Msg, byte_size(Data), PacketSize, WindowSize0, Acc0) of\n\t\t{WindowSize, Acc, {_, <<>>}} ->\n\t\t    {lists:reverse(Acc), Channel#channel{send_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t send_buf = NewBuffer}};\n\t\t{WindowSize, Acc, Rest} ->\n\t\t    get_window(Channel#channel{send_window_size = WindowSize,\n\t\t\t\t\t       send_buf = queue:in_r(Rest, NewBuffer)}, Acc)\n\t    end;\n\t{empty, NewBuffer} ->\n\t    {[], Channel#channel{send_buf = NewBuffer}}\n    end.\n\nhandle_send_window(Msg = {Type, Data}, Size, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) when Size =< WindowSize ->\n    case Size =< PacketSize of\n\ttrue ->\n\t    {WindowSize - Size, [Msg | Acc], {Type, <<>>}};\n\tfalse ->\n\t    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n\t    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}\n    end;\nhandle_send_window({Type, Data}, _, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) when WindowSize =< PacketSize ->\n    <<Msg1:WindowSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n    {WindowSize - WindowSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}};\nhandle_send_window({Type, Data}, _, PacketSize, WindowSize, Acc) ->\n    <<Msg1:PacketSize/binary, Msg2/binary>> = Data,\n    {WindowSize - PacketSize, [{Type, Msg1} | Acc], {Type, Msg2}}.\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Flow control\n%%% \n\nflow_control(Channel, Cache) ->\n    flow_control([window_adjusted], Channel, Cache).\n\nflow_control([], Channel, Cache) ->\n    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel),\n    [];\nflow_control([_|_], #channel{flow_control = From,\n\t\t\t     send_buf = Buffer} = Channel, Cache) when From =/= undefined ->\n    case queue:is_empty(Buffer) of\n\ttrue ->\n\t    ssh_client_channel:cache_update(Cache, Channel#channel{flow_control = undefined}),\n\t    [{flow_control, Cache, Channel, From, ok}];\n\tfalse ->\n\t    []\n    end;\nflow_control(_,_,_) ->\n    [].\n\n%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n%%%\n%%% Pseudo terminal stuff\n%%% \n\npty_req(ConnectionRef, Channel, Term, Width, Height,\n\t PixWidth, PixHeight, ?uint32(PixWidth),?uint32(PixHeight),\n\t\t\t\t    encode_pty_opts(PtyOpts)], TimeOut).\n\npty_default_dimensions(Dimension, TermData) ->\n    case proplists:get_value(Dimension, TermData, 0) of\n\tN when is_integer(N), N > 0 ->\n\t    {N, 0};\n\t_ ->\n            PixelDim = list_to_atom(\"pixel_\" ++ atom_to_list(Dimension)),\n\t    case proplists:get_value(PixelDim, TermData, 0) of\n\t\tN when is_integer(N), N > 0 ->\n\t\t    {0, N};\n\t\t_ ->\n\t\t    {?TERMINAL_WIDTH, 0}\n\t    end\n    end.\n\n-doc false.\nencode_pty_opts(Opts) ->\n    Bin = list_to_binary(encode_pty_opts2(Opts)),\n    <<?STRING(Bin)>>.\n\nencode_pty_opts2([]) -> \n    [?TTY_OP_END];\nencode_pty_opts2([{vintr,Value} | Opts]) ->\n    [?VINTR, ?uint32(Value) | encode_pty_opts2(Opts)];\nencode_pty_opts2([{vquit,Value}  100→%%====================================================================\n   101→\n   102→start_link(Role, Socket, Options) ->\n   103→    start_link(Role, undefined, Socket, Options).\n   104→\n   105→start_link(Role, Id, Socket, Options) ->\n   106→    case gen_statem:start_link(?MODULE,\n   107→                               [Role, Socket, Options],\n   108→                               [{spawn_opt, [{message_queue_data,off_heap}]}]) of\n   109→\n   110→        {ok, Pid} when Id =/= undefined ->\n   111→            %% Announce the ConnectionRef to the system supervisor so it could\n   112→            %%   1) initiate the socket handover, and\n   113→            %%   2) be returned to whoever called for example ssh:connect; the Pid\n   114→            %%      returned from this function is \"consumed\" by the connection\n   115→            %%      supervisor.\n   116→            ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(user_pid,Options) ! {new_connection_ref, Id, Pid},\n   117→            {ok, Pid};\n   118→\n   119→        Others ->\n   120→            Others\n   121→    end.\n   122→\n   123→takeover(ConnPid, Role, Socket, Options) ->\n   124→    case Role of\n   125→        client ->\n   126→            group_leader(group_leader(), ConnPid);\n   127→        _ ->\n   128→            ok\n   129→    end,\n   130→    {_, Callback, _} = ?GET_OPT(transport, Options),\n   131→    case Callback:controlling_process(Socket, ConnPid) of\n   132→        ok ->\n   133→            Ref = erlang:monitor(process, ConnPid),\n   134→            gen_statem:cast(ConnPid, socket_control),\n   135→            NegTimeout = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(negotiation_timeout,\n   136→                                           Options,\n   137→                                           ?GET_OPT(negotiation_timeout, Options)\n   138→                                          ),\n   139→            handshake(ConnPid, Role, Ref, NegTimeout);\n   140→        {error, Reason}\t->\n   141→            {error, Reason}\n   142→    end.\n   143→\n   144→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   145→-spec stop(connection_ref()\n   146→\t  ) -> ok | {error, term()}.\n   147→%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n   148→stop(ConnectionHandler)->\n   149→    case call(ConnectionHandler, stop) of\n   150→       {error, closed} ->\n   151→\t    ok;\n   152→\tOther ->\n   153→\t    Other\n   154→    end.\n   155→\n   156→%%====================================================================\n   157→%% Internal application API\n   158→%%====================================================================\n   159→\n   160→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   161→%%% Some other module has decided to disconnect.\n   162→\n   163→-spec disconnect(Code::integer(), Details::iodata(),\n   164→                      Module::atom(), Line::integer()) -> no_return().\n   165→%% error:{badkey,Key} ->\n   353→            undefined\n   354→    end;\n   355→retrieve(ConnectionHandler, Key) ->\n   356→    call(ConnectionHandler, {retrieve,Key}).\n   357→\n   358→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   359→%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n   360→set_sock_opts(ConnectionRef, SocketOptions) ->\n   361→    try lists:foldr(fun({Name,_Val}, Acc) ->\n   362→                            case prohibited_sock_option(Name) of\n   363→                                true -> [Name|Acc];\n   364→                                false -> Acc\n   365→                            end\n   366→                    end, [], SocketOptions)\n   367→    of\n   368→        [] ->\n   369→            call(ConnectionRef, {set_sock_opts,SocketOptions});\n   370→        Bad ->\n   371→            {error, {not_allowed,Bad}}\n   372→    catch\n   373→        _:_ ->\n   374→            {error, badarg}\n   375→    end.\n   376→\n   377→prohibited_sock_option(active)    -> true;\n   378→prohibited_sock_option(deliver)   -> true;\n   379→prohibited_sock_option(mode)      -> true;\n   380→prohibited_sock_option(packet)    -> true;\n   381→prohibited_sock_option(_) -> false.\n   382→\n   383→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   384→%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n   385→get_sock_opts(ConnectionRef, SocketGetOptions) ->\n   386→    call(ConnectionRef, {get_sock_opts,SocketGetOptions}).\n   387→\n 398→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   399→alg(ConnectionHandler) ->\n   400→    call(ConnectionHandler, get_alg).\n   401→\n   402→%%====================================================================\n   403→%% Intitialisation\n   404→%%====================================================================\n   405→init([Role, Socket, Opts]) when Role==client ; Role==server ->\n   406→    process_flag(trap_exit, true),\n   407→    %% ssh_params will be updated after receiving socket_control event\n   408→    %% in wait_for_socket state;\n   409→    D = #data{socket = Socket, ssh_params = #ssh{role = Role, opts = Opts}},\n   410→    {ok, {wait_for_socket, Role}, D}.\n   411→\n   412→%%%----------------------------------------------------------------\n   413→%%% Connection start and initialization helpers\n   414→init_connection_record(Role, Socket, Opts) ->\n   415→    {WinSz, PktSz} = init_inet_buffers_window(Socket),\n   416→    C = #connection{channel_cache = ssh_client_channel:cache_create(),\n   417→                    channel_id_seed = 0,\n   418→                    suggest_window_size = WinSz,\n   419→                    suggest_packet_size = PktSz,\n   420→                    requests = [],\n   421→                    options = Opts,\n   422→                    connection_supervisor = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(connection_sup, Opts)\n   423→                   },\n   424→    case Role of\n   425→        server ->\n   426→            C#connection{cli_spec =\n   427→                             ?GET_OPT(ssh_cli, Opts, {ssh_cli,[?GET_OPT(shell, Opts)]}),\n   428→                         exec =\n   429→                             ?GET_OPT(exec, Opts)};\n   430→        client ->\n   431→            C\n   432→    end.\n   433→\n   434→init_ssh_record(Role, Socket, Opts) ->\n   435→    %% Export of this internal function is\n   436→    %% intended for low-level protocol test suites\n   437→    {ok,PeerAddr} = inet:peername(Socket),\n   438→    init_ssh_record(Role, Socket, PeerAddr, Opts).\n   439→\n   440→init_ssh_record(Role, Socket, PeerAddr, Opts) ->\n   441→    AuthMethods = ?GET_OPT(auth_methods, Opts),\n   442→    S0 = #ssh{role = Role,\n   443→\t      opts = Opts,\n   444→\t      userauth_supported_methods = AuthMethods,\n   445→\t      available_host_keys = available_hkey_algorithms(Role, Opts),\n   446→\t      random_length_padding = ?GET_OPT(max_random_length_padding, Opts)\n   447→\t   },\n   448→\n   449→    {Vsn, Version} = ssh_transport:versions(Role, Opts),\n   450→    LocalName = case inet:sockname(Socket) of\n   451→                    {ok,Local} -> Local;\n   452→                    _ -> undefined\n   453→                end,\n   454→    case Role of\n   455→\tclient ->\n   456→\t    PeerName = case ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(host, Opts, element(1,PeerAddr)) of\n   457→                           PeerIP when is_tuple(PeerIP) ->\n   458→                               inet_parse:ntoa(PeerIP);\n   459→                           PeerName0 when is_atom(PeerName0) ->\n   460→                               atom_to_list(PeerName0);\n   461→                           PeerName0 when is_list(PeerName0) ->\n   462→                               PeerName0\n   463→                       end,\n   464→            ssh_lib:set_label(client,\n   465→                              {connection, list_to_binary(PeerName), Socket}),\n   466→            S1 =\n   467→                S0#ssh{c_vsn = Vsn,\n   468→                       c_version = Version,\n   469→                       opts = ?PUT_INTERNAL_OPT({io_cb, case ?GET_OPT(user_interaction, Opts) of\n   470→                                                            true ->  ssh_io;\n   471→                                                            false -> ssh_no_io\n   472→                                                        end},\n   473→                                                Opts),\n   474→                       userauth_quiet_mode = ?GET_OPT(quiet_mode, Opts),\n   475→                       peer = {PeerName, PeerAddr},\n   476→                       local = LocalName\n   477→                      },\n   478→            S1#ssh{userauth_pubkeys = [K || K <- ?GET_OPT(pref_public_key_algs, Opts),\n   479→                                            is_usable_user_pubkey(K, S1)\n   480→                                      ]\n   481→                  };\n   482→\n   483→\tserver ->\n   484→            ssh_lib:set_label(server, {connection, Socket}),\n   485→\t    S0#ssh{s_vsn = Vsn,\n   486→\t\t   s_version = Version,\n   487→\t\t   userauth_methods = string:tokens(AuthMethods, \",\"),\n   488→\t\t   kb_tries_left = 3,\n   489→\t\t   peer = {undefined, PeerAddr},\n   490→                   local = LocalName\n   491→\t\t  }\n   492→    end.\n   493→\n   494→handshake(ConnPid, server, Ref, Timeout) ->\n   495→    receive\n   496→\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n   497→\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n   498→\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n   499→\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n   500→\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n   501→\t    {error, Reason};\n   502→\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, {shutdown, Reason}} ->\n   503→\t    {error, Reason};\n   504→\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, Reason} ->\n   505→\t    {error, Reason};\n   506→        {'EXIT',_,Reason} ->\n   507→            stop(ConnPid),\n   508→            {error, {exit,Reason}}\n   509→    after Timeout ->\n   510→\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n   511→\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n   512→\t    {error, timeout}\n   513→    end;\n   514→handshake(ConnPid, client, Ref, Timeout) ->\n   515→    receive\n   516→\t{ConnPid, ssh_connected} ->\n   517→\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n   518→\t    {ok, ConnPid};\n   519→\t{ConnPid, {not_connected, Reason}} ->\n   520→\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n   521→\t    {error, Reason};\n   522→\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, {shutdown, Reason}} ->\n   523→\t    {error, Reason};\n   524→\t{'DOWN', Ref, process, ConnPid, Reason} ->\n   525→\t    {error, Reason}\n   526→    after Timeout ->\n   527→\t    erlang:demonitor(Ref, [flush]),\n   528→\t    ssh_connection_handler:stop(ConnPid),\n   529→\t    {error, timeout}\n   530→    end.\n   531→\n   532→handshake(Msg, #data{starter = User}) ->\n   533→    User ! {self(), Msg}.\n   534→\n   535→%%====================================================================\n   536→%% gen_statem callbacks\n   537→%%====================================================================\n   538→%%--------------------------------------------------------------------\n   539→-type event_content() ::  any().\n   540→\n   541→-type renegotiate_flag() :: init | renegotiate.\n   542→\n   543→-type state_name() :: \n   544→        {hello,                     role()                    }\n   545→      | {kexinit,                   role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n   546→      | {key_exchange,              role(), renegotiate_flag()}\n   547→      | {key_exchange_dh_gex_init,  server, {userauth_keyboard_interactive,       role()          }\n   554→      | {userauth_keyboard_interactive_extra, server          }\n   555→      | {userauth_keyboard_interactive_info_response, client  }\n   556→      | {connected,                 role()                    }\n   557→\t.\n   558→\n   559→%% The state names must fulfill some rules regarding\n   560→%% where the role() and the   566→\n   567→-define(CONNECTED(StateName),\n   568→        (element(1,StateName) == connected orelse\n   569→         element(1,StateName) == ext_info ) ).\n   570→\n   571→-spec handle_event(gen_statem:event_type(),\n   572→\t\t   event_content(),\n   573→\t\t   state_name(),\n   574→\t\t   #data{}\n   575→\t\t  ) -> gen_statem:event_handler_result(state_name()) .\n   576→\n   577→-define(CONNECTION_MSG(Msg),\n   578→        [{next_event, internal, prepare_next_packet},\n   579→         {next_event,internal,{conn_msg,Msg}}]).\n   580→\n   581→%% . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . . .\n   582→callback_mode() ->\n   583→    [handle_event_function,\n   584→     state_enter].\n   585→\n   586→%%% ######## {hello, client|server} ####\n   587→%% The very first event that is sent when ssh_connection_handler\n   588→%% becomes owner process for Socket\n   589→handle_event(cast, socket_control, {wait_for_socket, Role},\n   590→             #data{socket = Socket, ssh_params = #ssh{opts = Opts}}) ->\n   591→    case inet:peername(Socket) of\n   592→        {ok, PeerAddr} ->\n   593→            try\n   594→                {Protocol, Callback, CloseTag} = ?GET_OPT(transport, Opts),\n   595→                D = #data{starter = ?GET_INTERNAL_OPT(user_pid, Opts),\n   596→                          socket = Socket,\n   597→                          transport_protocol = Protocol,\n   598→                          transport_cb = Callback,\n   599→                          transport_close_tag = CloseTag,\n   600→                          ssh_params = init_ssh_record(Role, Socket, PeerAddr, Opts),\n   601→                          connection_state = init_connection_record(Role, Socket, Opts)\n   602→                         },\n   603→                NextEvent = {next_event, internal, socket_ready},\n   604→                {next_state, {hello,Role}, D, NextEvent}\n   605→            catch\n   606→                _:{error,Error} -> {stop, {error,Error}};\n   607→                error:Error ->     {stop, {error,Error}}\n   608→            end;\n   609→\n   610→        {error,Error} ->\n   611→            {stop, {shutdown,Error}}\n   612→    end;\n   613→\n   614→handle_event(internal, socket_ready, {hello,_}=StateName, #data{ssh_params = Ssh0} = D) ->\n   615→    VsnMsg = ssh_transport:hello_version_msg(string_version(Ssh0)),\n   616→    send_bytes(VsnMsg, D),\n   617→    case inet:getopts(Socket=D#data.socket, [recbuf]) of\n   618→\t{ok, [{recbuf,Size}]} ->\n   619→\t    %% Set the socket to the hello text line handling mode:\n   620→\t    inet:setopts(Socket, [{packet, line},\n   621→\t\t\t\t  {active, once},\n   622→\t\t\t\t  % Expecting the version string which                    StateName, D),\n   633→\t    {stop, {shutdown,{unexpected_getopts_return, Other}}}\n   634→    end;\n   635→\n   636→handle_event(internal, {info_line,_Line}, {hello,client}, D) keep_state_and_data;\n   641→\n   642→handle_event(internal, {info_line,Line}, {hello,server}=StateName, D) ->\n   643→    %% But the client may NOT send them to the server. Client sent info lines.\"}};\n   650→\n   651→handle_event(internal, {version_exchange,Version}, {hello,Role}, D0) ->\n   652→    {NumVsn, StrVsn} = ssh_transport:handle_hello_version(Version),\n   653→    case handle_version(NumVsn, StrVsn, D0#data.ssh_params) of\n   654→\t{ok, Ssh1} ->\n   655→\t    %% Since the hello part is finished correctly, we set the\n   656→\t    %% socket to the packet 691→\n   692→handle_event(internal, Msg = #ssh_msg_service_request{name=ServiceName}, StateName = {service_request,server}, D0) ->\n   693→    case ServiceName of\n   694→\t\"ssh-userauth\" ->\n
